L2018: Reviewer Comment: Strong CYP3A4 inhibitors should be avoided with lomitapide given this marked drug-drug interaction. The applicant has not investigated the effects of weaker CYP3A4 inhibitors, however, which may be more commonly prescribed and less-often recognized by clinicians for their drug-drug interaction potential.
L2019: The applicant's proposed labeling includes the following text under CONTRAINDICATIONS: "Concomitant use of [lomitapide] with moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors (e.g., diltiazem, fluconazole, erythromycin) or strong CYP3A4 inhibitors (e.g., antifungal azoles such as itraconazole or ketoconazole; macrolide antibiotics such as erythromycin or clarithromycin; ketolide antibiotics such as telithromycin; HIV protease inhibitors; or the antidepressant nefazodone."
L2020: 7.6 Additional Safety Evaluations
L2021: 7.6.1 Human Carcinogenicity
L2022: There have been no reports of cancer in the lomitapide development program, but this is not reassuring given the limited size and duration of the clinical trials. See Section 7.3.5 (p. 213) for additional discussion.
L2023: 7.6.2 Human Reproduction and Pregnancy Data
L2024: One HoFH subject in the lomitapide development program suspended therapy with lomitapide to plan a pregnancy. Her last dose of lomitapide treatment was 19 October 2010 and she became pregnant in January 2011. She had a normal delivery of a baby who appears healthy at present. The mother is not breastfeeding. The applicant reports that she restarted lomitapide 5 mg daily on 19 May 2012.
L2025: 7.6.3 Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
L2026: Lomitapide has not been studied in pediatric patients.
L2027: 7.6.4 Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal and Rebound
L2028: Overdose of lomitapide has not been reported. Lomitapide is not believed to have potential for drug abuse, and withdrawal/rebound effects have not been described.
L2029: 7.7 Additional Submissions / Safety Issues
L2030: Data from the 120-day safety update and from FDA information requests that occurred during the review have been integrated where appropriate.
L2031: 8 Postmarket Experience
L2032: None.
L2033: 9 Appendices

L2040: 9.2 Labeling Recommendations
L2041: My major labeling recommendations include the following:
L2042: • I recommend a boxed warning for the risk of hepatotoxicity to be consistent with the “Warnings and Precautions, Contraindications, and Boxed Warning Guidance,” which states that a boxed warning is included in labeling when FDA approves a drug with restricted distribution to ensure safe use.• Although lomitapide might reduce TG, hypertriglyceridemia is not a typical clinical feature of HoFH; the effect of reducing TG on clinical outcomes in HoFH is far from certain. I recommend removing this from the proposed indications. Given that there is a growing interest in following non-HDL-C, I would be in favor of adding non-HDL-C to the list of lipid parameter levels that lomitapide reduces.• Given that lomitapide was studied only in 29 subjects with HoFH at sufficient doses and duration, I recommend adding the following limitation of use: “The safety and effectiveness of lomitapide have not been established in patients with hypercholesterolemia who do not have HoFH.”• In general, I agree with the applicant’s proposed Dosage &amp; Administration schedule, but this section will need to be reorganized for clarity. In addition, I agree that daily supplements of vitamin E and fatty acids should be taken as the applicant proposes. As I mentioned in the review, it is unknown what effect lomitapide would have on these nutrient levels in the absence of supplements, and the optimal doses of supplement components are not known, but supplementation as performed in the pivotal/extension trials is the best available option given the available data.• Liver monitoring requires further discussion with our hepatology consultants. In general, I do not believe that prescriptive monitoring of liver fat should be specified in labeling since we cannot provide recommendations regarding when to modify or discontinue lomitapide for a given value (or change) in hepatic fat. One could consider measuring hepatic fat at baseline, but I do not believe that there is a hepatic fat level that would contraindicate one from attempting lomitapide for a patient with HoFH. One would have to suggest that liver monitoring (e.g., frequency) would be distinct for subjects with “high” levels of hepatic fat, but I would recommend monthly monitoring of liver-related tests for the first year even for patients with “low” levels of hepatic fat, and it would be unreasonable for routine monitoring to occur with greater frequency than this. Thus, I favor describing lomitapide-associated hepatic steatosis in labeling, along with the concern that it may lead to chronic liver disease, and allow practicing physicians to incorporate liver imaging into their individual management plans.• I suggest revising the contraindications to include: Pregnancy, Concomitant administration with moderate or strong CYP3A inhibitors, and Moderate or
L2043: severe hepatic impairment (i.e., Child-Pugh B and C) and patients with active liver disease). The applicant proposed additional contraindications, which I suggest incorporating elsewhere in the label for the following reasons:
L2044: ○ Patients with a known significant or chronic bowel disease such as inflammatory bowel disease or malabsorption. This is a theoretical risk. Chronic bowel diseases, including inflammatory bowel disease (e.g., ulcerative colitis), may not predispose to fat-soluble nutrient deficiency; therefore, for patients with HoFH and these conditions, benefit could certainly overcome the putative risk. Although I agree that patients with “malabsorption” may be at higher risk for these deficiencies, it is possible that increased supplementation may overcome the underlying problem depending on its severity. Thus, I recommend removing this as a contraindication and placing relevant text into the Warnings &amp; Precautions section where the effect of lomitapide on fat-soluble nutrients should be discussed.○ Concomitant administration of \geq 80 mg simvastatin. First, since lomitapide roughly doubles simvastatin exposure, patients should limit to 20 mg simvastatin or, in cases where they have already tolerated 80 mg simvastatin monotherapy for at least a year, they should limit to 40 mg simvastatin. These dose caps for simvastatin, however, do not belong in the Contraindications section; one could argue that they truly belong in simvastatin labeling since they describe simvastatin dosing instructions. Regardless, if they are to be included in the lomitapide labeling, I would place in Warnings &amp; Precautions and Drug Interactions.○ Patients with rare hereditary problems of galactose intolerance, the Lapp-lactase deficiency, or glucose-galactose malabsorption. This contraindication stems from the EMA guidelines (volume 3B) that address excipients in the label. The EMA guidelines state that for products containing lactose (&lt; 5 g), the proposal for the Summary of Product Characteristics (SPC) is “Patients with rare hereditary problems of galactose intolerance, the Lapp lactase deficiency or glucose-galactose malabsorption should not take this medicine.” This warning does not apply to individuals who have typical “lactose intolerance,” as they can typically tolerate 12-15 grams of lactose with minimal (or no) symptoms. Lomitapide contains 73.81 mg lactose per 5 mg capsule, 147.62 mg per 10 mg capsule, and 136.73 mg lactose per 20 mg capsule; therefore, the dose of lactose will remain well under 1 gram daily. The target population for this risk is those with congenital glucose-galactose malabsorption, which appears to have been described in approximately 300 individuals worldwide. These individuals typically have symptom onset within the first days of life because infants cannot tolerate milk, leading to extreme osmotic diarrhea, acidosis, volume depletion, and their consequences. I did find one drug (Zortress [everolimus]) that contains this statement in the Warnings &amp; Precautions section of U.S. labeling, and I would agree with this approach. I have not found other drugs with this statement listed as a Contraindication. Individuals with this severe, rare disorder from birth
L2045: would be well aware of checking all labels for lactose, and it appears that the initial symptoms would be osmotic diarrhea if lomitapide were taken by an affected individual, leading to osmotic diarrhea that would prompt discontinuation.
L2046: • I suggest the following Warnings &amp; Precautions: (1) Risk of hepatotoxicity (both elevated transaminases and hepatic steatosis); (2) Potential for embryo-fetal toxicity based on nonclinical data; (3) Reduced absorption of fat-soluble vitamins and serum fatty acids; (4) Gastrointestinal adverse reactions (given their nearly universal occurrence); (5) Drug-drug interaction with CYP3A inhibitors; (6) Potential for increased risk of myopathy with concomitant simvastatin (based on lomitapide increasing simvastatin exposure); (7) Drug-drug interaction with warfarin; and (8) use in individuals with rare hereditary disorders of glucose-galactose metabolism as discussed above.• Adverse reaction incidences require updating based on 78-week data, and some minor discrepancies in subject counts between my review of the final AE dataset and the 120-day safety update will require clarification.
L2047: •  (b) (4)
L2048: • Clinical Pharmacology input is required for the many drug-drug interactions (especially CYP3A) identified.• In the Clinical Studies section, additional detail is required regarding the trial and demographics; the figure depicting LDL-C over time could be improved (error bars, adding mean dose at each visit given the dose-titration regimen, using completers population);  (b) (4)
L2049: 9.3 Advisory Committee Meeting
L2050: The efficacy and safety of lomitapide mesylate for HoFH was discussed with the Endocrinologic and Metabolic Drugs Advisory Committee (EMDAC) on 17 October 2012. Meeting materials, slides, and transcripts can be found at www.fda.gov. A brief summary of the discussion and voting questions follows.
L2051: Discussion question #1 asked the committee to discuss whether the applicant has provided adequate evidence to support the efficacy of lomitapide (up to 60 mg per day) as an adjunct to a low-fat diet and other lipid-lowering drugs for the reduction of LDL-C
L2052: in patients with HoFH. The committee agreed that even though the phase 3 trial was uncontrolled and very small, the applicant provided adequate evidence to support a claim in this population.
L2053: Discussion question #2 asked the committee to provide an assessment of lomitapide's effect on HDL-C, TG, apo B, apo A1, and Lp(a). The committee noted that lomitapide's effects on all but HDL-C were in clinically desirable directions, and that the decrease in HDL-C was not worrisome, especially for this population that is characterized by extremely high LDL-C. The return of HDL-C to baseline provided some reassurance as well.
L2054: Discussion question #3 asked the committee to discuss whether LDL-C is an appropriate surrogate for reduced CV morbidity and mortality in lomitapide-treated patients with HoFH. The committee agreed that LDL-C was an appropriate surrogate for this orphan population, emphasizing that this recommendation was specific to the HoFH population.
L2055: Discussion question #4 asked the committee to discuss the liver-related effects observed in the lomitapide development program, including transaminase abnormalities and hepatic steatosis, and to discuss any concern regarding a possible association between hepatic steatosis and increased risk for CV morbidity and mortality. The committee expressed a moderate to high level of concern for the liver-related effects of lomitapide. The committee warned that the magnitude of transaminase elevations observed seems higher than one would expect from hepatic steatosis alone, suggesting the possibility of an alternative cause of the elevations. Although it was somewhat reassuring to the committee that transaminase levels and hepatic steatosis reversed when lomitapide was discontinued, they stressed that there is insufficient information regarding its long-term effects. Last, the committee was not concerned about a hypothetical link between hepatic steatosis and increased risk for CV morbidity/mortality.
L2056: Discussion question #4 also asked the committee to discuss monitoring for liver-related adverse effects, dosing recommendations based on quantitative thresholds of transaminase elevations or changes in hepatic steatosis, and population-based approaches to further characterize and assess liver safety post-approval. Although recommendations were heterogeneous, there was general consensus that full liver panels should be followed rather than only transaminases (i.e., include bilirubin, alkaline phosphatase, consider GGT). The committee expressed concerns about the variability in upper limits of normal across laboratories and suggested that absolute values are used instead (e.g., assuming 30 IU/L is the upper limit of normal for ALT, one might make a recommendation for dose reduction at 90 IU/L and for drug interruption at 150 IU/L). Regarding imaging of hepatic fat, a hepatologist stated that non-contrast MRI would need to be used, as ultrasound and CT are not quantitative. There was no agreement with regard to what level of hepatic fat might prompt changes to the dosing regimen or stopping the drug entirely. Some cautioned that the progression of liver disease is distinct in adults and children, and one might consider restricting lomitapide
L2057: to adults via the REMS. The committee acknowledged that there are a lot of uncertainties with regard to the liver risk, and that a liver-related death should be expected in the post-market setting.
L2058: Discussion question #5 asked the committee to discuss whether the applicant has sufficiently characterized the risk for deficiencies in fat-soluble vitamins and essential fatty acids and whether the applicant's proposal to mitigate this risk through recommending dietary supplements is adequate. The committee was generally agreeable with this approach, highlighting that vitamin E may benefit steatohepatitis as well. Some did express concern regarding the effects of lomitapide on these nutrients in developing children.
L2059: The voting question was, "Based on the information included in the briefing materials and presented today, has the applicant provided sufficient efficacy and safety data to support marketing of lomitapide for the treatment of HoFH?" Thirteen members voted in favor, two against. Most members who voted for approval emphasized that their vote was specifically for the HoFH population, and that the proposed REMS should be tightened beyond prescribers attesting that lomitapide is "medically appropriate" for a patient. The two who voted against were both pediatric hepatologists; they cited concerns regarding the adverse effects of lomitapide on the liver, use of lomitapide in children, and off-label use in non-HoFH adult populations. The committee also emphasized the importance of obtaining more data through a post-marketing observational study.
L2060: 9.4 Supplemental Tables and Figures
L2061: Phase 2 Non-HoFH Study Designs
L2062: Study CV145-009
L2063: Title: "The Effects of Chronic Dosing of BMS-201038 (lomitapide or AEGIR-733) on Hepatic Fat Accumulation and Reversibility as Assessed by Nuclear Magnetic Resonance Spectroscopy (NMRS)"
L2064: Dates: 20 Jan 1999 to 22 Dec 1999
L2065: Sponsors: Bristol-Myers Squibb Pharmaceutical Research Institute (prior to 22 Aug 2002); Daniel J. Rader, MD, University of Pennsylvania School of Medicine (22 Aug 2002 to 12 Apr 2007); Aegerion Pharmaceuticals, Inc. (13 Apr 2007 to present)
L2066: Objective: Compare the effects of lomitapide 25 mg daily x 4 weeks with matched placebo on the reversibility of hepatic fat content as measured by MRI/NMRS in subjects with hypercholesterolemia.
L2067: Size: 76 subjects in 5 sites in U.S.
L2068: Population: Healthy male or female subjects not of childbearing potential, age 18-65 years, with fasting LDL-C \geq 160 mg/dL and TG \leq 500 mg/dL (obtained off lipid-lowering agents after a lipid stabilization period; see below).
L2069: Withdrawal of lipid-lowering agents was required prior to the first qualifying lipid assessment (8 weeks for statins, niacin, or bile-acid sequestrants; 12 weeks for fibrates; 4 weeks for OTC treatments such as “herbal or natural” therapies; 1 year for probucol). Treatment with steroids (inhaled or oral), immunosuppressive agents, estrogens/progestogens (except stable HRT in post-menopausal women), androgens, and fish oil preparations within 8 weeks were exclusions.
L2070: Multiple exclusions, including (but not limited to) a history of diabetes mellitus (or fasting blood glucose &gt;140 mg/dL), an average ethanol consumption in excess of 14 drinks per week, CK &gt;1.5\times ULN of laboratory, or any obstructive hepatobiliary disease or other significant hepatic disease (defined as AST, ALT, GGT, total bilirubin, or alkaline phosphatase &gt;1.5\times ULN of laboratory).
L2071: Treatment Duration: 4 weeks (with follow-up 6 weeks after discontinuation)
L2072: Design:
L2073: • Stabilization: 4 weeks on a low-fat, low-cholesterol diet and non-lipid-lowering drugs prior to first lipid profile. Diet was a NCEP Step I diet, comprising 8-10% total calories from saturated fat, \leq 30\% of total calories from total fat, \leq 300 mg/day cholesterol.• Period A: 4 weeks of a placebo lead-in (Week A1 to A4)○ The mean fasting LDL-C at weeks A2 and A3 needed to be \geq 160 mg/dL to qualify, with the lower LDL-C within 15% of the higher value; if the two values differed by \geq 15\%, a third value could be obtained at week A4.• Period B: Randomized, double-blind, parallel-treatment involving 4 weeks of lomitapide 25 mg daily or matched placebo (1:1) (Week B1 to B4), followed by 6 weeks of drug discontinuation (Week B5 to B11)
L2074: Interventions:
L2075: • Lomitapide 25 mg daily or matched placebo (Lot numbers N97018 [lomitapide] and N97014 [placebo]).• Subjects were instructed to take their study drug in the morning, but relationship to food was not specified in the protocol.• Supplemental vitamins were not provided.
L2076: Primary Pharmacodynamic Assessment: Evaluation of the reversibility of fat accumulation in the liver based on MRI/NMRS radiologic imaging at baseline, end of treatment (after 4 weeks), and end of study (6 weeks post treatment).
L2077: Secondary Pharmacodynamic Assessments: Change in lipid profile from baseline (Week B1) to Weeks B2, B3, B4, and B5 (four weeks on treatment), and B11 (6 weeks post treatment). Lipid profile included TC, LDL-C, VLDL-C, HDL-C, apoB, Lp(a), and TG.
L2078: Safety Assessments: AEs (BMS categorization; not MedDRA), clinical laboratories (hematology, chemistry, urinalysis, coagulation parameters), vital signs, physical examinations, ECGs, and PFTs (spirometry and DLCO).
L2079: • Protocol amendment #3 (06 July 1999) added a discontinuation criterion for ALT &gt;3\times ULN as a result of 4 (10%) of 41 subjects randomized at the time having been discontinued because of abnormal transaminases as high as 643 U/L.
L2080: Interim Analysis: An interim analysis was performed by a panel of BMS staff after at least 20 subjects (\sim 1/3) had completed the full study to determine whether to continue the study. “For the NMRS results, clinical laboratory data, and SAEs, line listings and/or summary statistics by letter-coded treatment group were provided to the safety advisory panel as deemed necessary. The treatment decode was provided to the Vice-President-BDM by the randomization coordinator.”
L2081: Study AEG-733-001
L2082: Title: "A randomized, double-blind, active-controlled, parallel-group study to evaluate the safety and efficacy of the combination AEG-733 and ezetimibe vs. monotherapy in subjects with moderate hypercholesterolemia."
L2083: Dates: 30 June 2006 to 27 December 2006
L2084: Sponsors: Aegerion Pharmaceuticals, Inc.
L2085: Objective: Evaluate the efficacy of combination therapy (lomitapide force-titrated from 5 mg to 10 mg daily + ezetimibe 10 mg) vs. monotherapy of each agent on serum lipoproteins over 12 weeks of therapy. Characterize the safety and tolerability of these regimens.
L2086: Size: 85 subjects in 6 sites in U.S. (Sample size increased from 75 to 85 with protocol amendment 1 on 27 March 2006.)
L2087: Population: Men and women (with appropriate contraception if applicable), age 18-70 years, with fasting LDL-C \geq 160 and &lt; 250 mg/dL if 0-1 NCEP risk factors or \geq 130 and &lt; 250 mg/dL if \geq 2 NCEP risk factors or prior CHD, and TG \leq 400 mg/dL (obtained after a 4-wk washout period of lipid-lowering agents).
L2088: NCEP risk factors include cigarette smoking, hypertension (BP &gt; 140/90 or on antihypertensive medication), low HDL (&lt; 40 mg/dL), family history of premature CHD (CHD in first-degree male relative &lt; 55 years or female &lt; 65 years), age (men &gt; 45 years, women &gt; 55 years).
L2089: Multiple exclusions, including (but not limited to) a history of diabetes mellitus, cardiac insufficiency (functional NYHA class II-IV), history of regular alcohol use (&gt; 1 drink per day), acute cardiovascular event within previous 6 months, serum creatinine &gt; 2.5 mg/dL, or a history of liver disease or transaminases &gt; 1.5\times ULN at screening.
L2090: Treatment Duration: 12 weeks
L2091: Design:
L2092: • 3-arm, double-blind, active-controlled, parallel-group, randomized trial with equal allocation• Screening/Washout: 1-2-week screening period with 2 visits, where baseline lipids and other eligibility criteria were evaluated; this period also included a 4-week washout for subjects on prior lipid-lowering therapies. Subjects were counseled to implement a low-fat diet (&lt; 20\% calories from fat).• Treatment Period: 12 weeks (with interim visits at weeks 4 and 8 at the end of each lomitapide dose-escalation [see below])○ Arm 1 (lomitapide monotherapy): lomitapide + placebo-ezetimibe○ Arm 2 (ezetimibe monotherapy): placebo-lomitapide + ezetimibe 10 mg○ Arm 3 (combination therapy): lomitapide + ezetimibe 10 mg• Initial lomitapide daily dose was 5 mg in Arms 1 and 3, force-titrated to 7.5 mg at week 4, and to 10 mg at week 8. Final visit was at week 12. There was no scheduled off-treatment follow-up.

S2: 7.5 mg 733 + 10 mg apo

L2094: Figure 61. A-001 – Study Design
L2095: Source: A-001 CSR, p. 22.
L2096: Interventions:
L2097: • Lomitapide (force-titration 5 mg/day x 4 wk, 7.5 mg/day x 4 wk, 10 mg/day x 4 wk), ezetimibe 10 mg daily, or the combination. Matched placebos were used to blind in a double-dummy fashion. The clinical study report has lot numbers and expiration dates for lomitapide 5, 7.5, and 10-mg doses; ezetimibe; and matching placebos (A-001 CSR, Table 2).
• Subjects were instructed to take their study drugs each morning with a low-fat breakfast.
• Supplemental vitamins were not provided.
L2098: Primary Efficacy Endpoint: % change in calculated LDL-C after 12 weeks of therapy (visit 6). (LDL-C was estimated from the Friedewald equation unless TG &gt;400 mg/dL, in which case it was measured directly.)
L2099: Secondary Efficacy Endpoints:
L2100: • Absolute and % change from baseline to 12 weeks in each of the following: TC, non-HDL-C, TG, HDL-C, Lp(a), apoA1, apoB, hsCRP, body weight;
• % change from baseline for all efficacy endpoints at 4 and 8 weeks of therapy (i.e., after the 4-week dosing periods of 5 mg and 7.5 mg, respectively) (visits 4 and 5).
L2101: Safety Assessments: AEs (MedDRA v8.1), clinical laboratories (hematology, chemistry, urinalysis, coagulation parameters), vital signs, physical examinations, ECGs, and Gastrointestinal Symptoms Rating Scale (GSRS) self-administered questionnaire. Hepatic fat, fat-soluble nutrients, and pulmonary function were not assessed.
L2102: Interim Analysis: A pre-specified interim analysis was conducted on Week 8 data to evaluate the 5-mg and 7.5-mg doses to guide future development. At the time the interim analysis was conducted, however, the applicant states that the study had been completed.
L2103: Reviewer Comment: Although a 12-week phase 2 study, the maximum duration of exposure to the maximum tested dose of lomitapide was 4 weeks at 10 mg. Thus, the safety data obtained from this study is limited in its generalizability to the regimen proposed for the HoFH population.
L2104: Study AEG-733-003b (“A-003b”)
L2105: Title: “A randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel-group study to evaluate the safety and efficacy of the combination of AEG-733 (formerly BMS 201038) and atorvastatin 20 mg vs. monotherapy in subjects with moderate hypercholesterolemia.”
L2106: Dates: 05 November 2007 to 04 August 2008
L2107: Sponsors: Aegerion Pharmaceuticals, Inc.
L2108: Objectives: Evaluate the efficacy of combination therapy (fixed-dose lomitapide 5 mg or 10 mg + atorvastatin 20 mg) vs. placebo and monotherapy of each agent on serum lipoproteins over 8 weeks of therapy. Characterize the safety and tolerability of these regimens.
L2109: Size: 157 subjects in 17 sites in U.S.
L2110: Population: Men and women (with appropriate contraception if applicable), age 18-70 years, with fasting LDL-C \geq 160 and &lt; 250 mg/dL if 0-1 NCEP risk factors or \geq 130 and &lt; 250 mg/dL if \geq 2 NCEP risk factors or prior CHD, and TG \leq 400 mg/dL (obtained after a ~4-wk washout period of lipid-lowering agents).
L2111: NCEP risk factors include cigarette smoking, hypertension (BP &gt; 140/90 or on antihypertensive medication), low HDL (&lt; 40 mg/dL), family history of premature CHD (CHD in first-degree male relative &lt; 55 years or female &lt; 65 years), age (men &gt; 45 years, women &gt; 55 years).
L2112: Multiple exclusions, including (but not limited to) a history of diabetes mellitus, fasting glucose &gt; 110 mg/dL, cardiac insufficiency (functional NYHA class II-IV), history of regular alcohol use (&gt; 1 drink per day), acute cardiovascular event within previous 6 months, serum creatinine &gt; 2.5 mg/dL, history of liver disease or transaminases &gt; 1.5\times ULN at screening, positivity for hepatitis B or C, or BMI \geq 40 kg/m2.
L2113: Treatment Duration: 8 weeks
L2114: Design:
L2115: • 6-arm, double-blind, placebo- and active-controlled, parallel-group, randomized trial with equal allocation• Screening/Washout: 5-week screening period with an initial screening at day -35, when washout from other lipid-lowering medications began and when subjects were counseled to implement a low-fat/low-cholesterol diet ('old NCEP Step II diet, supplying &lt; 30\% kcal as fat, &lt; 7\% kcal as saturated fatty acid, and &lt; 200 mg dietary cholesterol). Follow-up screening visits were scheduled at day -10 and day -7.• Treatment Period: 8 weeks (with interim visits at weeks 1, 2, 4, and 6).○ Group A: Placebo daily○ Group B: atorvastatin 20 mg daily○ Group C: lomitapide 5 mg daily○ Group D: lomitapide 10 mg daily○ Group E: lomitapide 5 mg + atorvastatin 20 mg daily○ Group F: lomitapide 10 mg + atorvastatin 20 mg daily• Final visit was at week 8 (visit 8). There was no scheduled off-treatment follow-up.
L2116: Figure 62. A-003b – Study Design. A flow diagram showing the study design for A-003b. On the left, a box lists patient criteria: Men/women aged 18-70, Baseline LDL = 130-250 mg/dl, Baseline TGs &lt; 400 mg/dl. This box is connected to a central box labeled '156 Patients'. From this central box, seven horizontal bars represent different treatment groups, each with 26 patients: Placebo, 20 mg atorvastatin (ATV), 5 mg AEGIR-733, 10 mg AEGIR-733, 5 mg AEGIR-733 + 20 mg ATV, and 10 mg AEGIR-733 + 20 mg ATV. Below the bars, a timeline shows visits: D-35, D-10, D-7, D-0, W1, W2, W4, W6, W8. A double-headed arrow labeled '8 weeks' spans from D-0 to W8. A legend defines D = Day and W = Week.
L2117: Figure 62. A-003b – Study Design
L2118: Source: A-003b CSR, Figure 9-1.
L2119: Note: Actual N=157 (placebo n=27).
L2120: Interventions:
L2121: • Lomitapide (fixed-dose 5 mg or 10 mg daily), atorvastatin 20 mg daily, the combination, or placebo. Matched placebos were used to blind in a double-dummy fashion, and active atorvastatin was over-encapsulated.
• Subjects were instructed to take their study drugs in the evening at least 2 hours after dinner, with a low-fat snack.

○ The original protocol stated that subjects “will be instructed to take . . . study medication either: In the morning along with a low-fat breakfast OR in the evening at least 2 hours after dinner, with a low-fat snack.” As explained in response to an FDA information request, Aegerion amended the protocol, in a letter sent to sites in January 2008, to change the lomitapide dosing time for all subjects to the evening.


• Subjects were instructed to take a multivitamin tablet first thing in the morning. It does not appear that this was provided by the sponsor.
L2122: Primary Efficacy Endpoint: % change in calculated LDL-C after 8 weeks of therapy (visit 8). (LDL-C was estimated from the Freidewald equation unless TG &gt;400 mg/dL or the sample was non-fasting, in which case it was measured directly.)
L2123: Reviewer Comment: Although the CSR describes using the “ITT dataset” for the primary efficacy analysis, subjects who did not complete the treatment period and who did not have an 8-week LDL-C value were not included in the analysis (i.e., their data were not used in a repeated-measures model, for example). Furthermore, I note that “Blood draws for lipid and other efficacy measures (not safety) were only drawn if the study subject had received study medication in the past 3 days.” This design element precludes ITT analyses; instead, the only efficacy analyses possible will be akin to per-protocol analyses of completers. This certainly has potential to introduce bias.
L2124: Secondary Efficacy Endpoints:
L2125: • % changes from baseline to 4 and 8 weeks in each of the following: TC, non-HDL-C, TG, HDL-C, apoAI, apoB, hsCRP, body weight, and waist circumference;
• subgroup analyses by baseline TG (&gt;150 and ≤150 mg/dL) and BMI (&gt;30 and ≤30 kg/m2);
• % changes from baseline to weeks 1, 2, and 6 for all primary and secondary endpoints
L2126: Safety Assessments: AEs (MedDRA v10.1), clinical laboratories (hematology, chemistry, urinalysis, coagulation parameters), vitamins A and E, plasma insulin and C-peptide, hs-CRP, vital signs, physical examinations, ECGs, and Gastrointestinal Symptoms Rating Scale (GSRS) self-administered questionnaire. Hepatic fat and pulmonary function were not assessed.
L2127: Liver-related Safety:
L2128: Any subject who developed ALT and/or AST &gt;10x ULN and/or bilirubin &gt;2x ULN would have been removed from the study and followed until resolution or stabilization of these laboratory values.
L2129: Any subject with ALT and/or AST &gt;5x ULN, alkaline phosphatase &gt;3x ULN, or bilirubin &gt;1.5x ULN (unless evidence of underlying Gilbert's syndrome) would have the result(s) confirmed within 7-14 days. If repeat values remained greater than the above thresholds, study medication was to be discontinued until values decreased to ALT or AST &lt;2x ULN, alkaline phosphatase &lt;1.5x ULN, or bilirubin &lt;1.0x ULN. At that point, drug could be restarted.
L2130: Any subject with ALT and/or AST &gt;2x but &lt;5x ULN and otherwise asymptomatic (liver-related), and/or bilirubin &gt;1.0x but &lt;1.5x ULN (with normal pre-treatment levels) were to be carefully followed for further evaluation but would have continued on study drug.
L2131: Statistical Considerations:
L2132: • No sample size calculations were performed. "The sample size chosen for this study was based upon precedent set by other studies of similar nature...."• The primary efficacy null hypothesis was "There is no difference among the 6 treatment groups in the % change in LDL-C," and excluding the null with P \leq 0.05 prompted pairwise t-tests for each pair of treatments (i.e., 15 comparisons) without adjustment for multiplicity. Excluding the null with a one-way ANOVA was essentially certain when two of the six groups were placebo and atorvastatin 20 mg.
L2133: Study AEGR-733-004
L2134: Title: "A randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel-group study to evaluate low doses of the MTP inhibitor AEGR-733 on hepatic fat accumulation as measured by magnetic resonance spectroscopy."
L2135: Dates: 06 September 2007 to 05 September 2008
L2136: Sponsor: Aegerion Pharmaceuticals, Inc.
L2137: Objectives: Evaluate the impact of low-dose therapeutic regimens of lomitapide \pm atorvastatin, ezetimibe, or fenofibrate, compared with placebo, on hepatic fat accumulation (% hepatic fat) as measured by MRS. Briefly, secondary objectives were to evaluate these regimens on safety parameters and lipid profiles.
L2138: Size: 260 subjects (240 planned) in 16 sites in U.S.
L2139: Population: Men and women (with appropriate contraception if applicable, although women only relying on oral contraceptives were ineligible), age 18-70 years, with LDL-C \geq 100 and &lt; 190 mg/dL (after \sim 4-wk washout period of lipid-lowering agents), with screening hepatic fat &lt; 6.2\% (by MRS).
L2140: Multiple exclusions, including (but not limited to) a history of diabetes mellitus, cardiac insufficiency (functional NYHA class II-IV), history of regular alcohol use (&gt; 1 drink per day), acute cardiovascular event within previous 6 months, serum creatinine &gt; 2.5 mg/dL, history of liver disease or transaminases &gt; ULN at screening (allowed for retest if first screening value was within 10% of ULN), positivity for hepatitis B or C, or BMI &gt; 35 kg/m2.
L2141: Treatment Duration: 12 weeks
L2142: Design:
L2143: • 8-arm, double-blind, placebo-controlled, fixed-dose, parallel-group, randomized trial with equal allocation
L2144: • Screening/Washout: 5- to 9-week screening period with an initial screening visit to begin washout from other lipid-lowering medications and to counsel subjects to implement a low-fat/low-cholesterol diet (“old NCEP Step II diet, supplying &lt;30% kcal as fat, &lt;7% kcal as saturated fatty acid, and &lt;200 mg dietary cholesterol). Follow-up screening visits were scheduled at day -10 and day -7.
• Treatment Period: 12 weeks (with interim visits at weeks 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, and 10).
            
○ Group A: Placebo
○ Group B: Iomipamide 2.5 mg daily
○ Group C: Iomipamide 5 mg daily
○ Group D: Iomipamide 7.5 mg daily
○ Group E: Iomipamide 10 mg daily
○ Group F: Iomipamide 5 mg + atorvastatin 20 mg daily
○ Group G: Iomipamide 5 mg + micronized fenofibrate 145 mg daily
○ Group H: Iomipamide 5 mg + ezetimibe 10 mg


• Final visit was at week 12 (visit 11). There was no scheduled off-treatment follow-up.
• After randomization, all lipid measurements and MRS/MRI results were blinded to the investigator starting at Day 0/visit 4.

L2146: Figure 63. A-004 – Study DesignSource: A-004 CSR, Figure 1.Note: Actual N=160 (n=28 to 35 per group).
L2147: Interventions:
L2148: • Each subject received 3 capsules as follows:
L2149: Study ArmAEGR CapsulesComparator Capsule

H. AEGR-733 5 mg + ezetimibe 10 mgAEGR 2.5 mg × 2 +ezetimibe 10
L2150: • Atorvastatin, fenofibrate, and ezetimibe were over-encapsulated to maintain blinding, with matching placebos provided.
• Subjects were instructed to take their study drugs in the late evening along with a low-fat snack at least 2 hours after dinner.
• Subjects were instructed to take a multivitamin tablet each morning starting at Day 0 (baseline).
L2151: Primary Efficacy Endpoint: Hepatic fat content as measured by MRS at baseline and week 12.
L2152: Secondary Efficacy Endpoints:
L2153: • Hepatic fat content at week 4, 8, 12/End-of-study (LOCF) as measured by MRS
• Hepatic safety biomarkers (ALT, AST, alkaline phosphatase, total bilirubin) at baseline, weeks 4, 8, 12/end-of-study (LOCF)
• Occurrence of biopsy-proven hepatic steatosis
• Lipids (LDL-C, TC, TG, HDL-C), apoB, and apoAI at baseline, weeks 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12/end-of-study (LOCF)
• hs-CRP at baseline and week 12/end-of-study (LOCF)
• Weight and waist circumference at baseline, weeks 4, 8, 12/end-of-study (LOCF)
L2154: Safety Assessments: AEs (MedDRA v10.1; collected between date of first dose and 30 days after last dose), clinical laboratories (hematology, chemistry, urinalysis, coagulation parameters), vitamins A and E, plasma insulin and C-peptide (both added with protocol amendment on 04 April 2008), hs-CRP, vital signs, physical examinations, ECGs, Gastrointestinal Symptoms Rating Scale (GSRS) self-administered questionnaire, hepatic fat by MRS/MRI (screening, baseline, and weeks 4, 8, and 12), and pulmonary function tests (spirometry + DLCO at baseline and week 12).
L2155: Liver-related Safety:
L2156: Any subject who developed ALT and/or AST &gt;10x ULN and/or bilirubin &gt;2x ULN would have been removed from the study and followed until resolution or stabilization of these laboratory values.
L2157: Any subject with ALT and/or AST &gt;5x ULN, alkaline phosphatase &gt;3x ULN, or bilirubin &gt;1.5x ULN (unless evidence of underlying Gilbert's syndrome) would have the result(s) confirmed within 2-14 days. If repeat values remained greater than the above thresholds, study medication was to be discontinued until values decreased to ALT or AST &lt;2x ULN, alkaline phosphatase &lt;1.5x ULN, or bilirubin &lt;1.0x ULN. At that point, drug could be restarted.
L2158: Any subject with ALT and/or AST &gt;2x but &lt;5x ULN and otherwise asymptomatic (liver-related), and/or bilirubin &gt;1.0x but &lt;1.5x ULN (with normal pre-treatment levels) were to be carefully followed for further evaluation but would have continued on study drug.
L2159: Statistical Considerations:
L2160: • The primary endpoint analysis was a one-way ANOVA at week 12 to compare the absolute change from baseline in % hepatic fat (by MRS) between lomitapide 5 mg daily and placebo.• For secondary endpoints of change in hepatic fat from baseline to weeks 4, 8, and 12, each lomitapide monotherapy group was to be compared with placebo and each lomitapide 5 mg + atorvastatin/fenofibrate/ezetimibe group was to be compared with both placebo and lomitapide 5 mg monotherapy.• No sample size calculations were performed. "It was expected, however, that a sample size of 30 subjects per treatment group would yield sufficient data to provide reasonable point estimates and confidence intervals for the between-group differences in percent change in hepatic fat content."
L2161: Study AEG-733-006
L2162: Title: "A randomized, double-blind, comparator-controlled, parallel-group study to evaluate the safety and efficacy of the combination of AEG-733 and atorvastatin 20 mg vs. atorvastatin monotherapy in subjects with moderate hypercholesterolemia."
L2163: Dates: 12 May 2008 to 29 August 2008
L2164: Sponsor: Aegerion Pharmaceuticals, Inc.
L2165: Objectives: Evaluate the efficacy of combination therapy lomitapide + atorvastatin 20 mg versus atorvastatin monotherapy on LDL-C over 4 and 8 weeks of therapy. Briefly, secondary objectives were to evaluate the effects on other serum lipoproteins, hsCRP, body weight, markers of safety, and overall safety and tolerability.
L2166: Size: 44 subjects (50 planned) in 5 sites in U.S.
L2167: Population: Men and women (with appropriate contraception if applicable), age 18-70 years, with fasting LDL-C \geq 160 and &lt; 250 mg/dL if 0-1 NCEP risk factors or \geq 130 and &lt; 250 mg/dL if \geq 2 NCEP risk factors or prior CHD, and TG \leq 400 mg/dL (obtained after a ~5-wk washout period of lipid-lowering agents).
L2168: NCEP risk factors include cigarette smoking, hypertension (BP &gt; 140/90 or on antihypertensive medication), low HDL (&lt; 40 mg/dL), family history of premature CHD (CHD in first-degree male relative &lt; 55 years or female &lt; 65 years), age (men &gt; 45 years, women &gt; 55 years).
L2169: Multiple exclusions, including (but not limited to) a history of diabetes mellitus, fasting glucose &gt; 110 mg/dL, cardiac insufficiency (functional NYHA class II-IV), history of regular alcohol use (&gt; 1 drink per day), acute cardiovascular event within previous 6 months, serum creatinine &gt; 2.5 mg/dL, history of liver disease or transaminases &gt; 1.5 \times ULN at screening (one retest allowed), positivity for hepatitis B or C, or BMI \geq 40 kg/m2.
L2170: Treatment Duration: 8 weeks
L2171: Design:
L2172: • 2-arm, double-blind, randomized (blocks of 4), active-controlled, parallel-group trial with equal allocation• Screening/Washout: 5-week screening period with an initial screening at day -35, when washout from other lipid-lowering medications began and when subjects were counseled to implement a low-fat diet. Follow-up screening visits were scheduled at day -10 and day -7.• Treatment Period: 8 weeks (with interim visits at weeks 1, 2, 4, and 6)○ atorvastatin 20 mg daily○ atorvastatin 20 mg daily + lomitapide 2.5 mg daily, force-titrated to 5 mg daily at week 4• Final visit was at week 8 (visit 9). There was no scheduled off-treatment follow-up.

L2176: Interventions:
L2177: • Lomitapide 2.5 mg or 5 mg capsules (2.5 mg daily x 4 weeks followed by 5 mg daily x 4 weeks) or matching placebo capsules; atorvastatin 20 mg daily.
• Subjects were instructed to take their study drugs in the evening at least 2 hours after dinner, with a low-fat snack.
• The protocol states “Subjects should take a multivitamin first thing in the morning.” The clinical study report does not mention the use of multivitamins.
L2178: Primary Efficacy Endpoints: % change in LDL-C after 4 weeks of therapy (i.e., after lomitapide 2.5-mg dosing period) and % change in LDL-C after 8 weeks of therapy (i.e., after the lomitapide 5-mg dosing period).
L2179: Secondary Efficacy Endpoints: % change from baseline to 4 and 8 weeks in each of the following: TC, TG, HDL-C, non-HDL-C, apoB, apoA1, hsCRP, weight, waist circumference.
L2180: Safety Assessments: AEs (MedDRA v11.0), clinical laboratories (hematology, chemistry, urinalysis, coagulation parameters), vitamins A and E, plasma insulin and C-peptide, hs-CRP, vital signs, physical examinations, ECGs, and GSRS scores. Hepatic fat and pulmonary function were not assessed.
L2181: Liver-related Safety: Any subject who developed ALT and/or AST &gt;10x ULN and/or bilirubin &gt;2x ULN would have been removed from the study and followed until resolution or stabilization of these laboratory values.
L2182: Any subject with ALT and/or AST &gt;5x ULN, alkaline phosphatase &gt;3x ULN, or bilirubin &gt;1.5x ULN (unless evidence of underlying Gilbert’s syndrome) would have the result(s) confirmed within 2-4 days. If repeat values remained greater than the above thresholds, study medication was to be discontinued until values decreased to ALT or AST &lt;2x ULN, alkaline phosphatase &lt;1.5x ULN, or bilirubin &lt;1.0x ULN. At that point, drug could be restarted.
L2183: Any subject with ALT and/or AST &gt;2x but &lt;5x ULN and otherwise asymptomatic (liver-related), and/or bilirubin &gt;1.0x but &lt;1.5x ULN (with normal pre-treatment levels) were to be carefully followed for further evaluation but would have continued on study drug.
L2184: Statistical Considerations:
L2185: • No sample size calculations were performed. “The sample size chosen for this study was based upon precedent set by other studies of similar nature. . . .”
• The hypothesis to be tested for the primary efficacy endpoint was the between-group comparison in % change in LDL-C from baseline to week 8.

L2202: HoFH Phase 3 Supplemental Data
L2203: The following conventions apply for Figure 65 through Figure 70, which depict LDL-C profiles in HoFH-pivotal for subjects who either discontinued apheresis entirely or reduced the frequency of apheresis during the trial. The black solid line represents direct LDL-C values over time. The black triangles represent times of apheresis treatments. Gray vertical reference lines mark the subject's baseline visit and the primary efficacy endpoint at week 26. The lomitapide dose taken during the period immediately prior to the week 26 visit is noted in parentheses. Colored reference lines represent changes in concomitant lipid-lowering therapies: red lines indicate additions to therapy (including increases in dose) and blue lines indicate withdrawal of therapy; further details are provided in the footnotes for each change. The thick blue dashed lines mark the date of last apheresis or the approximate date of the last apheresis on the “typical” schedule for subjects who had the frequency reduced. I chose these dates post hoc based on qualitative assessment of apheresis intervals; they may or may not have corresponded to dates that the apheresis prescription was actually changed (not available on case report forms or datasets).

L2336: Background
L2337: Homozygous familial hypercholesterolemia (HoFH) is a rare, autosomal-dominant disease characterized by marked elevations in LDL-C. Individuals with this disease typically are homozygous or compound heterozygous for mutations in the LDL-receptor (LDL-R) gene, which leads to impaired LDL-R function. Because the LDL-R removes LDL particles from plasma via endocytosis, absent or defective LDL-R substantially reduces cellular uptake of LDL-C, leading to abnormally high levels of LDL-C in the systemic circulation. Extremely high levels of LDL-C put these individuals at high risk for early and severe cardiovascular disease: clinical manifestations, which typically present before the age of 20 years, include corneal arcus, xanthomata, and diffuse and accelerated atherosclerotic disease. Untreated, patients typically do not survive beyond 30 years as a result of a high incidence of cardiac death.
L2338: The mainstay of treatment for hypercholesterolemia in the general population, HMG-CoA reductase inhibition with statins, is only modestly effective in patients with HoFH. Statins reduce intracellular cholesterol, leading to increased expression of LDL-R, which enhances clearance of LDL-C from the plasma. Because patients with HoFH have severely attenuated LDL-R function, the efficacy of these drugs is impaired. Patients with HoFH, therefore, typical resort to LDL apheresis to reduce levels of LDL-C if this treatment is available; this extracorporeal treatment is often performed weekly to monthly since it only transiently reduces LDL-C. Other more-extreme treatment approaches have included portacaval shunting, ileal bypass surgery, and liver transplantation. All of these therapies carry significant morbidity and long-term consequences.
L2339: Lomitapide is a small-molecule inhibitor of microsomal triglyceride transfer protein (MTP), which is responsible for transferring triglycerides (TG) onto apolipoprotein B (apo B) during the assembly of VLDL, the precursor to LDL. Naturally occurring loss-of-function mutations in the MTP protein are responsible for the clinical condition abetalipoproteinemia, which is characterized by very low levels of plasma cholesterol, TG, and apo B. Lomitapide was developed to treat hypercholesterolemia, and because its mechanism of action does not rely on functional LDL-R, it was hypothesized to be an effective therapy for patients with HoFH. Notably, individuals with inherited abetalipoproteinemia can also exhibit marked fat malabsorption, acanthocytosis, hepatomegaly secondary to hepatic steatosis, and a deficiency in fat-soluble vitamins that results in atypical retinitis pigmentosa, coagulopathy, central and peripheral demyelination (e.g., posterior column neuropathy), and myopathy.

L2341: DateEvent / Notes



18 Jun 1996IND 50820 submitted by Bristol-Myers Squibb (BMS)
          
BMS-201038 for treatment of mixed dyslipidemia.



08 Jan 1997EOP1 Meeting

18 Feb 1998Meeting to discuss safety issues and clinical development plan

21 Aug 2002IND 50820 transferred to Daniel J. Rader, MD, University of Pennsylvania
          
Anticipated for treatment of HoFH



20 July 2004EOP2 Teleconference
L2342: DateEvent / Notes





Potential for DDI noted by the Division given that BMS-201038 appeared to be a potent CYP3A4 inhibitor. Given the potential for combination with other lipid-altering drugs in clinical practice, DDI studies with simvastatin, atorvastatin, rosuvastatin, and ezetimibe were considered necessary.
Concern about pulmonary phospholipidosis (PL) in chronic rodent toxicity studies without an established NOAEL. Dr. Rader noted that pulmonary function tests were performed in clinical studies and function was reported as normal.
DMEP asked for information about the metabolic enzyme responsible for the test drug.
DMEP noted that any expanded use of the product beyond HoFH would shift the risk-benefit profile of the development program.



27 Jan 2006OOPD Grant 1R01 FD003098-01 awarded for MTP Inhibitor for Familial Hypercholesterolemia (PI: Marina Cuchel, MD, PhD)

28 Aug 2006
Division provided comments regarding pivotal Phase 3 trial, "A phase III study of the MTP inhibitor BMS-201038 in patients with severe refractory hypercholesterolemia on current lipid lowering therapy."

PK/PD DDI studies with warfarin and a CYP2D6 substrate were highly recommended.



07 Feb 2007
EOP2 teleconference to discuss pivotal trial for HoFH indication

DMEP agreed that a proposed sample size of 25 subjects diagnosed with HoFH, all of whom would receive BMS-201038 at the maximum tolerated dose for a minimum of 52 weeks, should be adequate for the assessment of efficacy in the proposed population.
DMEP encouraged the sponsor to include "sufficient numbers of women and Asians in the study population given that the behavior and potential toxicity of the drug is likely [to] be enhanced in these populations."
DMEP agreed with a dose-titration scheme to enhance tolerability, but the proposed dosages did not provide adequate safety margins for pulmonary phospholipidosis, which remained a concern.
Discussion of DDI PK study designs
DMEP agreed that no placebo arm would be required given a proposed design that included a minimum 6-week run-in period for all subjects where concomitant lipid-lowering treatment must remain stable. The sponsor stated that the concomitant treatment was to remain stable for the first 26 weeks of the study. In addition, a 6-week follow-up period was added following the last dose of BMS-201038 when concomitant lipid-lowering therapy should remain stable. "In addition, the absence of the placebo group allows that all subjects will be exposed to a minimum of 52 weeks at the maximum tolerated dose of study drug."
DMEP: "The dose titration scheme allows for patients to be treated at their maximum tolerated dose for 8.5-17 months, depending on the duration of their individualized titration. The sponsor is urged to follow patients for one year after their maximum tolerated dose has been achieved." The sponsor concurred.
DMEP and the sponsor agreed to CK monitoring, measurement of beta-carotene, vitamin K levels (via carboxylation of serum osteocalcin), major metabolites, alpha-tocopherol (reported as Vitamin E/LDL or Vitamin E/HDL ratios, and further testing such as adipose-tissue sampling with abnormal ratios), and hepatic fat (1H MRS instead of MRI). Vitamin E supplementation would be increased to 400 IU daily.
The sponsor agreed to remove diabetes as an exclusion criterion.
Requirements for carcinogenicity, genotoxicity, and reproductive toxicology studies were discussed.
L2343: DateEvent / Notes





• DMEP suggested several pulmonary and hepatobiliary exclusions and prohibited concomitant medications (corticosteroids, betaine).



13 Apr 2007IND 50820 transferred to Aegerion for development of (now) AEGR-733 for "moderate hypercholesterolemia," which included severe refractory hypercholesterolemia

16 May 2007IND 77775 submitted by Daniel J. Rader, MD, University of Pennsylvania, for HoFH

07 June 2007
          Partial clinical hold (PCH) for IND 50820 (studies &gt;6 months duration)
          
• In October 2006, after consultation with the Division of Gastroenterology Products, the Division of Pulmonary and Allergy Products, and consideration of the pharm/tox review of the pre-clinical data, all MTP inhibitors – with the exception of IND 77775 – were placed on PCH for clinical protocols &gt;6 months duration because "insufficient information is available to assess the risk for pulmonary phospholipidosis with long-term use of the drug." Sponsors were asked to submit a final report from a completed 3-month, repeat-dose rat toxicology study that included a recovery group and a sufficient number of active doses to establish a NOAEL for pulmonary PL. The protocols were to include electron microscopy. IND 77775 was not placed on PCH because the risk-benefit ratio was considered distinct for the HoFH population.



23 Oct 2007Orphan drug designation for the treatment of HoFH (Designation 07-2459)

28 Feb 2008IND 77775 transferred to Aegerion from Dr. Rader to facilitate conducting multi-site trials

10 Aug 2009
          Teleconference with Aegerion in lieu of premature EOP2 meeting
          
• At an 05 Aug 2009 internal meeting preceding a scheduled 12 Aug 2009 EOP2 teleconference, DMEP determined that lingering concerns regarding PL and the failure to study lomitapide in combination with an optimal statin dose in severe refractory hypercholesterolemia precluded an EOP2 meeting.
• Dr. Eric Colman informed representatives from Aegerion that the mouse carcinogenicity study results, which would include data on pulmonary PL, would need to be submitted before a discussion of phase 3 studies in the severe refractory hypercholesterolemia population.



09 Nov 2009
          Type B Face-to-face EOP2 Meeting with Aegerion
          
• Mouse carcinogenicity data: malignant tumors in the small intestine and liver at high doses. Data had not been reviewed in detail by the Agency (submitted 26 Oct 2009). The rat carcinogenicity study was ongoing, but reportedly no tumors had been observed. DMEP stated that the rat results would be important in determining the overall safety implications for humans; "At minimum, the carcinogenicity findings with the respective systemic exposure multiples would be included in the label, if the drug is eventually approved."
• Pulmonary PL: DMEP had less concern given that a number of approved compounds (e.g., fluoxetine) were identified that were associated with pulmonary PL in animal studies without known pulmonary dysfunction in humans. However, Aegerion was encouraged to resolve this issue from a pre-clinical perspective.
• Hepatic steatosis: DMEP recommended a 6-month safety study to observe lomitapide's effect on hepatic fat, hepatic structure/function, and insulin sensitivity. Exploratory endpoints such as IL-6, CRP, CK18 fragments, and enhanced liver fibrosis (ELF) panel were suggested. The sponsor cited results from study 733-004 to answer safety questions regarding hepatic fat accumulation with lomitapide use; in this 12-week study in ~250 patients, hepatic fat was measured four times and the
L2344: DateEvent / Notes




company stored samples that could be used to measure biomarkers.

The sponsor stated their intent to submit an NDA if the rat carci study was negative and the Agency allowed them to focus on the refractor HeFH population, with appropriate liver monitoring. DMEP replied that additional information would be needed in the broader dyslipidemic population given that the drug would invariably be used by this population, even if approved for a very limited indication. The sponsor was encouraged to submit an updated development plan; this was submitted 18 Nov 2009.
In response to pre-clinical questions about fecal fat, the sponsor submitted human study CV145002 report on 18 Dec 2009; on 29 Dec 2009, the agency told the firm that these data were insufficient to address the question of fecal fat excretion with lomitapide because the study was not adequately conducted.



18 Feb 2010Partial clinical hold lifted

17 May 2010
Type B Face-to-face EOP2 Meeting

Several clinical pharmacology comments were relayed to the sponsor prior to this meeting, including clarification of the dosing of lomitapide with respect to meals, requiring a DDI study with warfarin, requiring an investigation of the inhibition/induction potential of the major metabolites (such as M1 and M3) on major CYP enzymes in in vitro and possibly in vivo systems, and a renal impairment study. The sponsor was asked to justify why only the metabolites M1, M2, and M3 were to be monitored given that M8 was the most abundant metabolite.
At this meeting, Aegerion informed DMEP that they were only pursuing the HoFH indication as a result of financial constraints. Aegerion was amenable to “whatever post-approval supply constraints were necessary to ensure that the drug was available only to the HoFH population.”
(b) (4)



28 Jul 2010
Follow-up teleconference to discuss NDA limited to HoFH

“At the time of submission, all patients will have been treated for a minimum of 56 weeks. This was found acceptable by the Agency.”
Clinical pharmacology concerns were still outstanding. In addition to those mentioned previously, it was also a concern that lomitapide exposure was higher in women than in men. Furthermore, since the DDI study with simvastatin did not use the maximum dose of lomitapide, a separate DDI study with simvastatin and 60-mg lomitapide was recommended.



03 Mar 2011Orphan drug designation for the treatment of familial chylomicronemia

05 Apr 2011Type B pre-NDA CMC meeting

14 Apr 2011Fast-track designation request submitted

14 Jun 2011Fast-track designation denied, as the development program was not designed to determine whether the product will reduce cardiovascular morbidity or mortality in the HoFH population or lower-risk populations

15 Jun 2011
Type B Face-to-face pre-NDA Meeting (Clinical questions only)

The sponsor was encouraged to characterize all metabolites.
Regarding CYP3A4 DDIs, the sponsor “proposed to address the CYP3A4 interaction in labeling; the package insert will indicate to avoid taking lomitapide with moderate or mild CYP3A4 inhibitors in addition to strong CYP3A4 inhibitors. The agency agreed with this approach. The
L2345: DateEvent / Notes




firm was encouraged to use simulations to evaluate the effect of mild and moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors on lomitapide. The labeling language will be a review issue. It was also strongly recommended that the sponsor address the in-vitro induction potential of lomitapide and its major metabolites on major CYP enzymes."

• The Agency agreed to the sponsor's proposal to conduct a population PK analysis later in development by combining data from pediatric studies.
• The Agency confirmed that a single, pivotal phase 3 study lacking a placebo control arm would not preclude filing or approval of the lomitapide NDA for the HoFH population.
• The Agency agreed that the available exposure data from the single pivotal phase 3 study (UP1002/733-005) are sufficient to support an NDA for HoFH. Briefly, the sponsor stated that data would be available for 18 patients who had been exposed for at least 1.5 years and 10 who had been exposed for at least 2 years; maximum tolerated dose for patients receiving treatment for at least 2 years would range from 20 mg to 60 mg.
• The Agency noted that including a "functional HoFH" definition of average fasting LDL &gt; 300 mg/dL on maximally tolerated lipid-lowering therapy closely resembles the severe refractory HeFH population, which would shift the risk-benefit ratio. "Dr. Rader recognized that the treatment indication for lomitapide will need to align with the inclusion criteria of the Phase 3 trial. Additionally, Dr. Rader understood that inclusion of patients on maximal tolerated therapy with LDL &gt;300 mg/dL for treatment with lomitapide (which was not an inclusion criterion in the Phase 3 trial) is a REMS issue that will be addressed as part of the NDA review process."
• Regarding a potential REMS, the sponsor was encouraged to submit all planned materials that would be necessary to implement their proposal and to provide detailed plans of how distribution would be restricted to the HoFH population studied in the Phase 3 trial, including how documentation of HoFH status would be collected and confirmed, how distribution would be accomplished, and how the system would be monitored for compliance.
• CDISC SDTM and ADaM datasets would be submitted
• The sponsor stated that the primary data to support the effectiveness and safety would come from the phase 3 study UP1002/733-005 with supportive data from UP1001 (HoFH, phase 2) and 5 other phase 2 studies that involved subjects with elevated LDL-C. The Agency agreed with the general approach.
• The Agency accepted the proposed dates of database locks as long as adverse events of special interest that occurred afterward would be included in the safety update prior to filing the NDA.
• Analyses and tables to facilitate the clinical review were requested (e.g., Hy's Law cases, categorical laboratory-related analyses, review of reasons for dropout/discontinuation, analyses of changes in concomitant medications, correlations between compliance with dietary fat and adverse events, analyses regarding the number and frequency of adverse events depending on dose of concomitant statin, analyses comparing efficacy with and without LDL apheresis, data presentation to facilitate site selection for inspection).

The applicant expects that approval would ultimately trigger the 7-year orphan exclusivity provision. The applicant included four patents owned by The Trustees of the University of Pennsylvania in Module 1.3.5.1.
L2358: Site Inspections
L2359: The safety and efficacy database for the HoFH population comprises one phase 2 study (UP1001) conducted by Dr. Daniel Rader (University of Pennsylvania) and one single-arm, open-label phase 3 pivotal study (UP1002/733-005). The phase 3 study included a total of 29 subjects at 11 sites (2 U.S.; 3 South Africa; 2 Canada; 4 Italy).
L2360: Each phase 3 study site contributed between 1 and 5 subjects. A preliminary review of AEs, SAEs, deaths, and discontinuations do not reveal any obvious site-level outliers. The FDA Site Selection Tool (v2.2) suggests the following three sites have the highest "total risk:" site 01 (U.S.), 11 (S. Africa), and 12 (S. Africa). These three sites enrolled 14 of the study's 29 subjects. With the exception of one Canadian site that enrolled 3 subjects, all other sites enrolled 1 or 2 subjects. The only other site in the U.S. (site 02) enrolled 2 subjects.
L2361: Because there is only one pivotal phase 3 trial, I suggest inspection of the two sites in S. Africa that collectively contributed nine (31%) of the study's 29 subjects.
L2362: Furthermore, because of the relatively limited clinical experience of lomitapide in the HoFH population, for whom higher dosages were used than those used for other hypercholesterolemic subjects studied in the development program, I suggest inspection of the only site that conducted a phase 2 study in the HoFH population (Dr. Daniel Rader, University of Pennsylvania).
